<a id="open-group"></a>
## The Open Group

原文：The Open Group。PDF 物理页：[7]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

The Open Group 是全球联盟，通过技术标准与开源举措帮助实现业务目标，并在其 900 多个成员组成的多元群体中培育协作、包容与相互尊重的文化。成员包括多个行业的客户、系统及解决方案供应商、工具厂商、集成商、学界人士和顾问。

The Open Group 的使命是通过以下方式推动无边界信息流（Boundaryless Information Flow™）的实现：

- 与客户合作，捕获、理解并处理当前和新出现的需求，制定政策，分享最佳实践。
- 与供应商、联盟及标准组织合作，形成共识、促进互操作，演进并整合规范与开源技术。
- 提供全面服务，以提高联盟的运营效率。
- 开发并运营业界领先的认证服务，鼓励采购经过认证的产品。

更多信息见 www.opengroup.org。

The Open Group 出版广泛的技术文档，多数聚焦标准与指南的开发，也包括白皮书、技术研究、认证及测试文档与商业类出版物。详情见 www.opengroup.org/library。

**G217:p0007:c001 · PDF 7**

The Open Group

**G217:p0007:c002 · PDF 7**

The Open Group is a global consortium that enables the achievement of business objectives through technology standards and open source initiatives by fostering a culture of collaboration, inclusivity, and mutual respect among our diverse group of 900+ memberships. Our membership includes customers, systems and solutions suppliers, tool vendors, integrators, academics, and consultants across multiple industries.

**G217:p0007:c003 · PDF 7**

The mission of The Open Group is to drive the creation of Boundaryless Information Flow™ achieved by:

**G217:p0007:c004 · PDF 7**

• Working with customers to capture, understand, and address current and emerging requirements, establish policies, and share best practices

**G217:p0007:c005 · PDF 7**

• Working with suppliers, consortia, and standards bodies to develop consensus and facilitate interoperability, to evolve and integrate specifications and open source technologies

**G217:p0007:c006 · PDF 7**

• Offering a comprehensive set of services to enhance the operational efficiency of consortia

**G217:p0007:c007 · PDF 7**

• Developing and operating the industry’s premier certification service and encouraging procurement of certified products

**G217:p0007:c008 · PDF 7**

Further information on The Open Group is available at www.opengroup.org.

**G217:p0007:c009 · PDF 7**

The Open Group publishes a wide range of technical documentation, most of which is focused on development of Standards and Guides, but which also includes white papers, technical studies, certification and testing documentation, and business titles. Full details are available at www.opengroup.org/library.

</details>

<a id="this-document"></a>
## 本文档

原文：This Document。PDF 物理页：[7, 8]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

本文档是关于在数字企业中使用 TOGAF® 标准的 TOGAF® 系列指南，由 The Open Group 制定并批准。本文档旨在回答两个总体问题：

1. 企业架构与 TOGAF 标准如何使能数字企业？
2. 何时及如何应用 TOGAF 方法和最佳实践，引导数字企业经历其发展阶段——《数字从业者知识体系》标准将其称为涌现模式（emergence mode）？

本文档的高层结构如下：

- 第 1 章从既有企业架构实践如何为各种规模的数字企业带来价值的角度，概述本文档。
- 第 2 章描述企业架构与 TOGAF 标准如何为各种规模的数字企业带来有价值的工具。
- 第 3 章对齐 TOGAF 标准与《数字从业者知识体系》中的术语。
- 第 4 章详细说明如何将企业架构与 TOGAF 标准应用于 DPBoK™ 标准描述的语境。
- 附录 A 列出企业架构收益。
- 附录 B 列出 TOGAF 标准中的原则。
- 附录 C 展示企业架构服务如何将 TOGAF 活动打包为内部中心与客户中心两大类别，以按需交付价值。
- 附录 D 进一步说明 TOGAF 标准与 DPBoK 标准之间的联系。

本文档面向承担企业架构师及数字从业者角色的人。对数字从业者，它说明哪些架构实践有助于发展数字企业，以及如何与企业架构群体协作来获得这些实践。对承担企业架构师角色的人，它提供支持数字企业的指导。

面向两类具有不同文化与方法的读者，还有一项附带收益：分享各自群体的信息，以促进合作和富有成效的互动。


### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0007:c012 · PDF 7**

This Document

**G217:p0007:c013 · PDF 7**

This document is a TOGAF® Series Guide to Using the TOGAF® Standard in the Digital Enterprise. It has been developed and approved by The Open Group. This document sets out to answer two overarching questions:

**G217:p0007:c014 · PDF 7**

1. How do Enterprise Architecture and the TOGAF Standard enable the digital enterprise?

**G217:p0007:c015 · PDF 7**

2. When and how to apply TOGAF methods and best practices to guide a digital enterprise through its stages of development, which the Digital Practitioner Body of Knowledge™ Standard refers to as the emergence mode?

**G217:p0008:c000 · PDF 8**

The high-level structure of this document is as follows:

**G217:p0008:c001 · PDF 8**

• Chapter 1 provides a high-level introduction to this document in terms of how established Enterprise Architecture practices bring value to digital enterprises at all scales

**G217:p0008:c002 · PDF 8**

• Chapter 2 describes how Enterprise Architecture and the TOGAF® Standard bring valuable tools to digital enterprises of all sizes

**G217:p0008:c003 · PDF 8**

• Chapter 3 provides an alignment of terminology between the TOGAF Standard and the Digital Practitioner Body of Knowledge

**G217:p0008:c004 · PDF 8**

• Chapter 4 provides details on applying Enterprise Architecture and the TOGAF Standard to the contexts described in DPBoK™ Standard

**G217:p0008:c005 · PDF 8**

• Appendix A lists Enterprise Architecture benefits

**G217:p0008:c006 · PDF 8**

• Appendix B lists principles from the TOGAF Standard

**G217:p0008:c007 · PDF 8**

• Appendix C shows how Enterprise Architecture services package TOGAF activities to deliver value on demand in two major categories: internal-centric and customer-centric

**G217:p0008:c008 · PDF 8**

• Appendix D further illustrates the connections between the TOGAF Standard and the DPBoK Standard

**G217:p0008:c009 · PDF 8**

The audience for this document is those undertaking the roles of both Enterprise Architects and Digital Practitioners. For Digital Practitioners it communicates what architecture practices would help to grow their digital enterprise, and how to interact with the Enterprise Architecture community to get them. For those undertaking an Enterprise Architect role it provides guidance on supporting the digital enterprise.

**G217:p0008:c010 · PDF 8**

A side benefit, therefore, of addressing two audiences, each with different cultures and approaches, is sharing information about each community to facilitate cooperation and productive engagements.

</details>

<a id="series-guides"></a>
## 关于 TOGAF® 系列指南

原文：About the TOGAF® Series Guides。PDF 物理页：[8]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

TOGAF® 系列指南提供如何使用 TOGAF 标准以及如何调整它以满足特定需要的指导。

TOGAF® 系列指南预计是 TOGAF 标准中发展最快的部分，定位为标准的指导部分。TOGAF 基础内容预计长期稳定，而使用标准的指导可针对特定行业、架构风格、目的及问题。例如，为支持扩展企业转型所需的利益相关者、关注点、视图及支持模型，可能与支持内部 IT 环境向云迁移的内容显著不同；但二者都使用 ADM，都从架构愿景开始，并在形成实施与迁移计划的过程中开发目标架构。TOGAF 基础内容始终是跨行业、域及风格的基本框架。


### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0008:c011 · PDF 8**

About the TOGAF® Series Guides

**G217:p0008:c012 · PDF 8**

The TOGAF® Series Guides contain guidance on how to use the TOGAF Standard and how to adapt it to fulfill specific needs.

**G217:p0008:c013 · PDF 8**

The TOGAF® Series Guides are expected to be the most rapidly developing part of the TOGAF Standard and are positioned as the guidance part of the standard. While the TOGAF Fundamental Content is expected to be long-lived and stable, guidance on the use of the TOGAF Standard can be industry, architectural style, purpose, and problem-specific. For example, the stakeholders, concerns, views, and supporting models required to support the transformation of an extended enterprise may be significantly different than those used to support the transition of an in-house IT environment to the cloud; both will use the Architecture Development Method (ADM), start with an Architecture Vision, and develop a Target Architecture on the way to an Implementation and Migration Plan. The TOGAF Fundamental Content remains the essential scaffolding across industry, domain, and style.

</details>

<a id="authors"></a>
## 关于作者

原文：About the Authors。PDF 物理页：[9, 10]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

**Terence Blevins**

Terence Blevins 是 The Open Group 会士（Fellow），Enterprise Wise LLC 的所有者，也是一位半退休的企业架构师。他目前是 The Open Group 理事会（Governing Board）的董事。自 20 世纪 80 年代担任 NCR Corporation 战略架构总监以来，他一直参与架构学科的发展。Terence 自 1996 年首次接触架构论坛（Architecture Forum）起便参与 The Open Group 的工作。他曾担任架构论坛联合主席，并经常为 TOGAF 材料（包括业务场景方法，Business Scenario Method）做出贡献。他曾任 The Open Group 的副总裁兼 CIO，为 The Open Group“无边界信息流™”（Boundaryless Information Flow™）愿景做出了贡献。他拥有扬斯敦州立大学（Youngstown State University）数学专业的学士学位与硕士学位。

**Andy Ruth**

Andy Ruth 于 20 世纪 70 年代以技术专家身份开始 IT 职业生涯，先后在咨询与内部职员岗位任职。在接下来的二十年里，他在技术序列中不断晋升，从架构师一路升至管理层，最终成为管理者的管理者。20 世纪 90 年代中期，他从交付 IT 能力转向交付培训：在会议上演讲，并撰写书籍与培训课程，内容涵盖 IT 各个领域。20 世纪 90 年代末，Andy 加入 Microsoft，创建 Microsoft 认证架构师（Microsoft Certified Architect）项目，并负责咨询团队的架构师角色管理。近几年，Andy 运用其才华为架构社区发展标准、培训与工具。业余时间，他管理一个学徒计划，帮助他人进入职场或发展职业生涯。

**Heidi Hasz**

Heidi Hasz 在 IT 架构领域拥有 25 年以上经验，参与过从效率提升、成本削减到全新建设（green fields）的各种举措。Heidi 在加利福尼亚的 IBM 开始其 IT 架构职业生涯，帮助《财富》500 强客户建立企业架构及企业架构实践。1998 年，IBM 将她调往荷兰，领导跨境企业基础设施优化项目。在 IBM 任职期间，她是 IBM IT 架构全球知识中心的成员，负责开发和交付 IBM 架构方法论与知识资本库。目前，Heidi 是 TOGAF 培训师与架构课程开发者，正在参与一个数字化赋能、经认证的 TOGAF 课程的首次发布工作。

**Sonia Gonzalez**

Sonia Gonzalez 是 The Open Group 的 TOGAF 产品经理。她在不同领域与行业垂直领域拥有 25 年以上业务与企业架构顾问经验。Sonia 的经验包括业务与战略咨询以及企业架构与解决方案咨询，应用了不同的框架、最佳实践与工具。

Sonia 持有 The Open Group 颁发的以下认证：TOGAF 9 认证与 ArchiMate® 3 实践者（Practitioner）。她还获得了 The Open Group 的以下开放徽章（Open Badges）：TOGAF® Essentials 2018、TOGAF® 标准 9.2 版以及 TOGAF® 企业架构建模实践者 9.2。其他认证包括 COBIT® 5 认证、COBIT® 认证培训师、SAFe® 4 认证、AIPMM 认证产品经理，以及 BPM 与 BPMN™ 课程。

**G217:p0009:c000 · PDF 9**

About the Authors

**G217:p0009:c001 · PDF 9**

Terence Blevins

**G217:p0009:c002 · PDF 9**

Terence Blevins, a Fellow of The Open Group, is the owner of Enterprise Wise LLC and is a semi-retired Enterprise Architect. He is currently a Director of The Open Group Governing Board. He has been involved with the architecture discipline since the 1980s when he was Director of Strategic Architecture at NCR Corporation. Terence has been involved with The Open Group since 1996 when he first was introduced to the Architecture Forum. He was co-chair of the Architecture Forum and a frequent contributor of TOGAF material, including the Business Scenario Method. Terence was Vice- President and CIO of The Open Group where he contributed to The Open Group Vision of Boundaryless Information Flow™. He holds undergraduate and Masters degrees in Mathematics from Youngstown State University.

**G217:p0009:c003 · PDF 9**

Andy Ruth

**G217:p0009:c004 · PDF 9**

Andy Ruth started his IT career in the 1970s as a technical expert in both consulting and staff roles. Over the next two decades he rose up through the technical ranks, from the role of architect and through the ranks of management to become a manager of managers. In the mid-1990s, he shifted from delivering IT capability to delivering training; speaking at conferences, and writing books and training for covering the IT space. Toward the end of the 1990s, Andy joined Microsoft to create the Microsoft Certified Architect program and to manage the architect role for the consulting group. In the last few years, Andy has employed his talents to grow standards, training, and tools for the architecture community. In his spare time, he manages an apprenticeship program that helps others to enter the workforce or grow their career.

**G217:p0009:c005 · PDF 9**

Heidi Hasz

**G217:p0009:c006 · PDF 9**

Heidi Hasz has more than 25 years of experience in IT Architecture, working on initiatives from efficiency improvements and cost takeout to green fields. Heidi began her IT architecture career in California for IBM where she helped Fortune 500 clients to establish an Enterprise Architecture and an Enterprise Architecture practice. In 1998, IBM transferred her to The Netherlands to lead cross-border enterprise infrastructure optimization engagements. During her time at IBM, she was a member of the IBM IT architecture worldwide knowledge center responsible for developing and delivering the IBM architecture methodology and intellectual capital repository. Currently, Heidi is a TOGAF® trainer and architecture course developer working on the initial release of a digitally-enabled accredited TOGAF course.

**G217:p0009:c007 · PDF 9**

Sonia Gonzalez

**G217:p0009:c008 · PDF 9**

Sonia Gonzalez is The Open Group TOGAF Product Manager. She has more than 25 years of experience as a Business and Enterprise Architecture Consultant in different fields and industry verticals. Sonia’s experience includes Business and Strategy Consultancy as well as Enterprise Architecture and Solutions Consultancy, applying different frameworks, best practices, and tools.

**G217:p0009:c009 · PDF 9**

Sonia holds the following certifications issued by The Open Group: TOGAF® 9 Certified and ArchiMate

**G217:p0009:c010 · PDF 9**

®

**G217:p0010:c000 · PDF 10**

®

**G217:p0010:c001 · PDF 10**

3 Practitioner. She also has earned the following Open Badges from The Open Group: TOGAF Essentials 2018, TOGAF® Standard, Version 9.2, and TOGAF® Enterprise Architecture Modeling Practitioner 9.2. Other certifications include COBIT® 5 Certified, COBIT Certified Trainer, SAFe® 4 Certified, AIPMM Certified Product Manager, and courses in BPM and BPMN™.

</details>

## 1. 引言

原文：1. Introduction。PDF 物理页：[17]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

本文档回答两个总体问题：

1. 企业架构与 TOGAF 标准 [C220] 如何使能数字企业？
2. 何时以及如何应用 TOGAF 方法与最佳实践，引导数字企业走过其发展阶段——DPBoK™ 标准 [C196] 称之为“涌现模型”（emergence model）？

TOGAF 标准支持战略决策与架构规格的可持续交付，帮助组织成为数字企业；它可用于 DPBoK 标准的**个人/创始人、团队、团队之团队、持久企业**四个语境，以提供信息并做出决策。TOGAF 标准的实施始终按使用它的组织的规模、文化与运营方式裁剪。

本文档提供如何调整标准以支持数字企业的洞见，并与 DPBoK 标准的概念与涌现模型语境保持一致。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 本指南以 DPBoK 的个人/创始人、团队、团队之团队、持久企业四个语境裁剪 TOGAF，以支持决策及架构规格的可持续交付。
  - 适用边界：四语境描述规模与复杂性；不等同于其他指南的四种架构工作目的，也不要求统一部署所有 ADM 制品。
  - PDF 17：The TOGAF Standard supports strategic decision-making and a sustainable delivery of architecture specifications to support organizations to become a digital enterprise. It can be used in the Individual/Founder, Team, Team of Teams, and Enduring Enterprise contexts in the DPBoK™ Standard to inform and make decisions. The implementation of the TOGAF Standard is always adapted to fit the scale, culture, and operation of the organization in which it is used.

### 原文内容单元

1. Introduction

**G217:p0017:c001 · PDF 17**

This document sets out to answer two overarching questions:

**G217:p0017:c002 · PDF 17**

1. How do Enterprise Architecture and the TOGAF® Standard [C220] enable the digital enterprise?

**G217:p0017:c003 · PDF 17**

2. When and how to apply TOGAF methods and best practices to guide a digital enterprise through its stages of development, which the Digital Practitioner Body of Knowledge™ Standard [C196] refers to as the emergence model?

It can be used in the Individual/Founder, Team, Team of Teams, and Enduring Enterprise contexts in the DPBoK™ Standard to inform and make decisions. The implementation of the TOGAF Standard is always adapted to fit the scale, culture, and operation of the organization in which it is used.

**G217:p0017:c005 · PDF 17**

This document provides insight on how to adapt the standard to support the digital enterprise in

**G217:p0017:c006 · PDF 17**

**G217:p0017:c007 · PDF 17**

alignment with the DPBoK Standard concepts and contexts of the emergence model.

<a id="1.1"></a>
## 1.1 概述

原文：1.1. Overview。PDF 物理页：[17, 18]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

本文档描述企业架构（采用 TOGAF 标准定义的方法）如何以及何时可用于使能数字企业。TOGAF 标准中收集与编纂的经验与知识，来自全球各地多年来构建并运营公司 IT 部分的人士；这些经验教训可用于减少成长中的数字企业可能遭受的“成长阵痛”。

文档结构采用 DPBoK 标准的四个语境，描述数字产品与数字型公司可能经历的不同阶段或成熟度水平：

- 第 1 章介绍企业架构师与数字从业者及其视角，以及连接二者的战略；文档采用“前瞻”（peek-ahead）方法，使从一个语境成熟到下一个语境的数字企业能够恰当应用 TOGAF 标准与企业架构，“向前看”为语境转换做准备；还描述通过服务交付模型交付企业架构的重要性——即“企业架构即服务”策略。
- 第 2 章描述为什么数字企业应应用基于 TOGAF 标准的企业架构，并说明如何用其反应式与主动式地管理与避免技术债务；还描述当数字产品成熟、需要运营卓越时，二者如何使产品与公司成熟。
- 第 3 章澄清 TOGAF 标准与 DPBoK 标准使用的术语，并指出 The Open Group 对文档中所用术语的常用定义。
- 第 4 章是主体，回顾 DPBoK 标准的四个语境，描述企业架构在每个语境中可贡献什么，以及支持各语境的企业架构原则、能力与服务；最后说明 TOGAF ADM 的哪些部分与 TOGAF 系列指南适用于每个语境。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 前瞻是在当前语境中为下一语境的转换准备适量架构支持。
  - 适用边界：不能把可能的未来复杂性当作立即部署全部企业级流程的依据。
  - PDF 17：Chapter 1 provides an introduction to the Enterprise Architect and the Digital Practitioner along with their perspectives and the strategy to connect them. The document presents guidance using a “peek- ahead” approach so a digital enterprise maturing from one context to the next can appropriately apply the TOGAF Standard and Enterprise Architecture to “look ahead” in preparation for transitioning from context to context. It also describes the importance of delivering Enterprise Architecture through a service delivery model – the Enterprise Architecture as a service strategy.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0017:c008 · PDF 17**

1.1. Overview

**G217:p0017:c009 · PDF 17**

This document describes how and when Enterprise Architecture, using the approach defined in the TOGAF Standard, can be used to enable a digital enterprise. The experience and knowledge collected and cataloged in the TOGAF Standard has come from years of experience by people around the globe who have built and run the IT portions of companies around the world. The lessons learnt can be used to minimize the growing pains from which maturing digital enterprises will likely suffer.

**G217:p0017:c010 · PDF 17**

The structure of this document uses the four contexts and descriptions from the DPBoK Standard to describe the different stages or maturity levels through which a digital product and digitally-based company might transition.

**G217:p0017:c011 · PDF 17**

Chapter 1 provides an introduction to the Enterprise Architect and the Digital Practitioner along with their perspectives and the strategy to connect them. The document presents guidance using a “peek- ahead” approach so a digital enterprise maturing from one context to the next can appropriately apply the TOGAF Standard and Enterprise Architecture to “look ahead” in preparation for transitioning from context to context. It also describes the importance of delivering Enterprise Architecture through a service delivery model – the Enterprise Architecture as a service strategy.

**G217:p0017:c012 · PDF 17**

Chapter 2 describes why Enterprise Architecture using the TOGAF Standard should be applied in a digital enterprise, and describes using each to reactively and proactively manage and avoid technical debt. It also describes how, as digital products mature and operational excellence is needed, both can be used to mature a product and a company.

**G217:p0017:c013 · PDF 17**

Chapter 3 provides clarity for the terminology used by the TOGAF Standard and the DPBoK Standard. It also points out definitions commonly used by The Open Group for any terms used in the document that the reader might want to review.

**G217:p0018:c000 · PDF 18**

Chapter 4 comprises the bulk of the content as it reviews the four contexts used by the DPBoK Standard and describes what Enterprise Architecture can contribute during each context. It also describes the Enterprise Architecture principles, capabilities, and services that support each context. Finally, the chapter describes which parts of the TOGAF ADM and TOGAF Series Guides apply to each context to assist with further guidance.

</details>

<a id="1.2"></a>
## 1.2 数字从业者与企业架构师

原文：1.2. The Digital Practitioner and the Enterprise Architect。PDF 物理页：[18, 19]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

DPBoK 标准描述运营成功数字企业（含数字产品交付）所需的技能。数字技术与数字工作方式结合产生的新业务模式正在改变全球经济与社会；数字投资对现代组织至关重要。参与其交付（即为实现价值而创建和管理它们）可为个人、社区及公私企业带来繁荣。全球学习项目面临压力，需培养更多合格专业人员以满足旺盛的劳动力需求；过去 20 年技能要求发生巨变：数字从业者需要广泛技能与胜任力，包括云架构与运营、持续交付与部署、协作、敏捷与精益方法、产品管理等。

DPBoK 标准旨在支持数字从业者的成长，既为数字劳动力新进入者提供指导，也帮助有经验的从业者更新理解，看清数字与 IT 管理的各类主题与组件在新世界中如何组合。企业架构师可用 DPBoK 标准理解文化、领导实践与产品开发，并据此与产品团队建立融洽关系，发现可以在何处向产品团队提供架构服务；也可用该标准更好地理解会创建哪些制品，以及在 DPBoK 标准的涌现模型语境中如何采集这些制品。

每位数字从业者都希望并期待自己的企业大获成功，按照 DPBoK 标准涌现模型所述，从创始人状态指数级成长为持久企业。但当企业成长后，面对跨多个团队、甚至跨多条业务线协调的复杂性时，会发生什么？当与每个业务伙伴或客户的每份合同都需要法律审查，当他们面临合规与监管问题，或成为诉讼目标时，又会发生什么？

数字从业者可以把本文档以及其中指向 TOGAF 标准所定义具体方法的指引，当作一种“前瞻”工具。本文档提供的指导帮助数字从业者完成 DPBoK 标准中定义的企业架构任务；它指出/建议当前最佳实践，使具有长期后果的决策在信息充分的基础上做出，并在企业成长为拥有数百或数千名员工的大型企业时为其成功做好准备。随着公司与团队成长并做出影响其产品的决策，他们可以利用前几代从业者来之不易的经验教训。通过审视 TOGAF 标准在何处提供工具、模板与流程来完成 DPBoK 标准所讨论的任务，他们将获得通过明智决策成功成长所需的工具。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 架构师先理解产品团队的文化、领导和开发方式，再确定服务与已有制品的采集方式。
  - 适用边界：已有制品可以复用；不能仅因模板不同就要求产品团队重新制作同义材料。
  - PDF 18：The DPBoK Standard is intended to support the development of the Digital Practitioner. It seeks to provide guidance for both new entrants into the digital workforce as well as experienced practitioners seeking to update their understanding on how all the various themes and components of digital and IT management fit together in the new world. The Enterprise Architect can use the DPBoK Standard to understand culture, leadership practices, and product development. They can use that information to create a rapport with product teams and discover where they can provide an architectural service to product teams. They can also use this standard to better understand what artifacts are created and how to harvest them in the context of the emergence model from the DPBoK Standard.

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0018:c001 · PDF 18**

1.2. The Digital Practitioner and the Enterprise Architect

**G217:p0018:c002 · PDF 18**

The DPBoK Standard describes the skills needed to operate a successful digital enterprise, including digital product delivery.

**G217:p0018:c003 · PDF 18**

New business models resulting from a combination of digital technology, combined with digital ways of working, are transforming economies and societies worldwide. Digital investments are critical for modern organizations. Participating in their delivery (i.e., working to create and manage them for value) can provide prosperity for individuals, communities, and public and private enterprises. Learning programs worldwide are under pressure to produce an increasing number of qualified professionals to meet voracious workforce demands. Skill requirements have undergone a seismic shift over the past 20 years; Digital Practitioners require a wide variety of skills and competencies, including cloud architecture and operations, continuous delivery and deployment, collaboration, Agile and Lean methods, product management, and more.

**G217:p0018:c004 · PDF 18**

The DPBoK Standard is intended to support the development of the Digital Practitioner. It seeks to provide guidance for both new entrants into the digital workforce as well as experienced practitioners seeking to update their understanding on how all the various themes and components of digital and IT management fit together in the new world. The Enterprise Architect can use the DPBoK Standard to understand culture, leadership practices, and product development. They can use that information to create a rapport with product teams and discover where they can provide an architectural service to product teams. They can also use this standard to better understand what artifacts are created and how to harvest them in the context of the emergence model from the DPBoK Standard.

**G217:p0018:c005 · PDF 18**

Every Digital Practitioner wants and expects their company to be a wild success and to grow exponentially from a founder state to an enduring enterprise as described in the DPBoK Standard emergence model. But what happens when, as it grows, they are faced with the complexity of coordination across multiple teams and potentially across multiple lines of business? What happens when every contract with every business or customer needs a legal review, when they face compliance and regulatory concerns, or when they are the target of litigation?

**G217:p0018:c006 · PDF 18**

The Digital Practitioner can use this document and the pointers to specific methods defined in the TOGAF Standard as a sort of “peek-ahead” tool. This document provides guidance that helps the Digital Practitioner complete the Enterprise Architecture tasks that are defined in the DPBoK Standard. It indicates/suggests current best practices so that decisions with long-term consequences are well- informed and set for success as they mature to a large enterprise with several hundred or thousand employees. As the company and teams grow and make decisions that impact their product, they can leverage the hard-earned lessons from the generations of practitioners that came before them. By reviewing where the TOGAF Standard has tools, templates, and processes to complete the tasks

**G217:p0019:c000 · PDF 19**

discussed in the DPBoK Standard, they will have tools necessary to grow successfully through well- informed decisions.

</details>

<a id="1.2.1"></a>
## 1.2.1 语境与背景

原文：1.2.1. Context and Background。PDF 物理页：[19, 20]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

数字从业者与企业架构师的视角常常不同。而且，由于目标并不一致，他们的工作方式可能在两者之间引入冲突。例如，数字从业者的目标是尽快交付客户价值，基于客户反馈快速学习与更新，并尽可能减少该学习循环之外的努力与注意力。企业架构师的目标则是了解并维护关于其运营生态的足够信息，以便尽可能提供足够且恰当的信息，支持领导层做出明智决策，并向产品团队提供正确、有依据的指导。

TOGAF 标准与 DPBoK 标准各自在两个不同的计算时代演进。这已经够有挑战性了，而我们同时还处于业务极度颠覆的时期。这种颠覆的驱动因素之一，是计算与带宽（包括移动设备）价格的急剧下降。这代表了以远低于过去的成本交付数字价值的机会。企业看到了向客户提供数字产品的价值；或者，即使它们没有看到价值，也看到了如果不拥抱数字产品将面临的竞争劣势，因为竞争对手会利用数字交付成本的下降。你甚至会考虑在一家没有移动应用的银行办理银行业务吗？你会想到乘坐一家不提供在线预订与值机的航空公司吗？

企业架构与 TOGAF 标准开始演进时，使用的是带大型系统的本地数据中心，互联网、虚拟机环境与云资源才刚刚发展。软件与硬件都要花钱，采购周期长，而且需要放入必须规划空间、电力与制冷的数据中心。一切都必须正确规划与执行，预期业务成果才能成为现实。在可能的情况下，购买软件优于构建软件，这样商品化 IT 能力的代码库就是别人的问题，产品团队可以专注于提供竞争优势的特性。实验与快速（或任何）失败通常意味着举措失败。如果举措在启动后被取消，就确实存在已购资产从未产生收入流的风险。

如今，数字产品与服务已成为企业走向市场的主要方式。DPBoK 与 TOGAF 标准都支持数字产品时代。创始人与产品负责人对其市场有清晰的数字价值主张。他们的产品团队拥有并专注于较小的微服务，这些微服务集成到更大的解决方案中；通过在其他解决方案中复用组件，能够快速响应市场反馈。特性发布时带有应用编程接口（API），以便实现自动化与轻松集成。相关想法与创新主要来自两个来源：

- 产品团队必须包含一名或多名对其所工作的系统与软件有深入了解的人员；了解当前技术部署能做什么，以及技术债务在何处阻碍特性发布
- 产品团队必须直接从最终用户收集洞见

这些变化与互联网、虚拟机、可租用的云资源、开源软件与工具的主导地位，以及大多数行业与企业中司空见惯的高速创新结合在一起。交付数字能力的产品团队首先关注代码产出。代码必须转化为最终用户价值。任何使注意力偏离开发与交付代码及新特性的事情，都被视为错失的机会。前期规划与通过最终用户反馈驱动产品开发是预期之中的。实验与快速失败不仅受到鼓励，也是预期之中的。

在更快开发更好数字产品的积极面之外，还有一些后果需要关注。今天的数字产品可能成为明天的技术债务。构建数字产品时不考虑某些全局，可能导致意外后果；例如隐私与安全泄露，以及高昂的集成与互操作成本。

在本文档中，我们向任何从由内而外视角转向“由外而内”视角、从项目转向产品重点、或采用更敏捷与精益的软件交付方法的人，提供企业架构指导。本文档为实践企业架构的人提供重新思考其方法所需的洞见。他们应自问：所要求的每项流程与制品是否都是为数字企业带来价值所必需的，还是过去组织与实践的遗留物。本文档为数字从业者提供利用企业架构成功完成 DPBoK 标准所述步骤与任务、同时管理风险所需的指导。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 架构信息须足够且适当，以支撑领导决策和有依据的产品指导。
  - 适用边界：足够不是最大化数据或文档数量。
  - PDF 19：The perspectives of the Digital Practitioner and the Enterprise Architect are often different. Moreover, they may operate in ways that introduce conflict between them due to goals that are at odds. For instance, the goal of the Digital Practitioner is to deliver customer value as quickly as possible, learning and updating rapidly based on customer feedback, and minimizing effort and attention outside of this learning cycle as much as possible. The goal of the Enterprise Architect is to know and maintain enough information about the ecosystem in which they operate to provide enough of the right information as possible for leadership to make well-informed decisions, and provide correct and informed guidance to product teams.
- 对每个要求的流程与制品检查其为数字企业带来的价值。
  - 适用边界：历史惯例不能单独构成保留流程或强制产物的理由。
  - PDF 20：In this document we provide Enterprise Architecture guidance to anyone shifting from an inside-out view to an “outside-in” view, from a project to product focus, or adopting more Agile and Lean approaches to software delivery. This document provides those practicing Enterprise Architecture with the insights needed to rethink their approach. They should ask themselves if each process and artifact they require is necessary to bring value to the digital enterprise, or if it is a legacy of past organizations and practices. It provides the Digital Practitioner with the guidance needed to leverage Enterprise Architecture to successfully complete the steps and tasks described in the DPBoK Standard while managing risks.

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0019:c001 · PDF 19**

1.2.1. Context and Background

**G217:p0019:c002 · PDF 19**

The perspectives of the Digital Practitioner and the Enterprise Architect are often different. Moreover, they may operate in ways that introduce conflict between them due to goals that are at odds. For instance, the goal of the Digital Practitioner is to deliver customer value as quickly as possible, learning and updating rapidly based on customer feedback, and minimizing effort and attention outside of this learning cycle as much as possible. The goal of the Enterprise Architect is to know and maintain enough information about the ecosystem in which they operate to provide enough of the right information as possible for leadership to make well-informed decisions, and provide correct and informed guidance to product teams.

**G217:p0019:c003 · PDF 19**

The TOGAF Standard and the DPBoK Standard each evolved during two different computing eras. That is challenging enough, but we are also in a time of extreme business disruption. One of the drivers of this disruption is the steep fall in price of computing and bandwidth, including in mobile devices. This represents an opportunity to deliver digital value at dramatically lower costs than in the past. Businesses see the value of having digital products to offer to their customers. or, even if they do not see the value, they see the competitive disadvantage they will face if they do not embrace digital products, as their competitors will take advantage of the fall in cost of digital delivery. Would you even consider banking with a bank that did not have a mobile app? Would you ever think of flying with an airline that did not offer online reservation and check in?

**G217:p0019:c004 · PDF 19**

Enterprise Architecture and the TOGAF Standard began to evolve at a time when on-premise data centers with large systems were in use and the Internet, virtual machine environments, and cloud- based resources were just evolving. Software and hardware cost money, had long cycles for procurement, and needed to be placed in data centers that had to plan for space, power, and cooling. Everything had to be planned and executed correctly for the intended business outcome to become reality. When available, buying software was preferred to building software so the code base for commodity IT capability was someone else’s problem and the product teams could focus on features that provided a competitive advantage. Experimentation and quick (or any) failure typically meant failure of the initiative. If an initiative was canceled after it was started, there was a very real risk of having purchased assets that never created a revenue stream.

**G217:p0019:c005 · PDF 19**

We have now entered an age where digital products and services are a primary way the enterprise goes to market. The DPBoK and TOGAF Standards both support the age of digital products. Founders and product owners have a clear digital value proposition for their market. Their product teams own and focus on smaller microservices that are integrated into larger solutions. These offer the ability to rapidly respond to market feedback through reuse of their components in larger solutions. When a feature is released, it has an Application Programming Interface (API) so that automation and easy integration is possible. Relevant ideas and innovation come primarily from two sources:

**G217:p0019:c006 · PDF 19**

• The product team must contain one or more people with a deep knowledge of the systems and the software they work on; what is possible with their current technology deployment, and where the technical debt inhibits feature release

**G217:p0020:c000 · PDF 20**

• The product team must collect insights directly from the end users

**G217:p0020:c001 · PDF 20**

These changes are combined with the dominance of the Internet, virtual machines, cloud-based resources for rent, open source software and tools, and innovation occurring at break-neck speed being commonplace in most industries and businesses. Product teams delivering digital capability focus on code output above all else. The code must translate to end-user value. Anything that takes the focus away from developing and delivering code and new features is considered a lost opportunity. Upfront planning and driving product development through end-user feedback are expected. Experimentation and quick failure are not only encouraged, but also expected.

**G217:p0020:c002 · PDF 20**

Along with the positives of developing better digital products more quickly, there are some consequences that need attention. Today’s digital products can become tomorrow’s technical debt. Digital products built without consideration of some big picture can result in unintended consequences; for example, privacy and security breaches, and high costs of integration and interoperability.

**G217:p0020:c003 · PDF 20**

In this document we provide Enterprise Architecture guidance to anyone shifting from an inside-out view to an “outside-in” view, from a project to product focus, or adopting more Agile and Lean approaches to software delivery. This document provides those practicing Enterprise Architecture with the insights needed to rethink their approach. They should ask themselves if each process and artifact they require is necessary to bring value to the digital enterprise, or if it is a legacy of past organizations and practices. It provides the Digital Practitioner with the guidance needed to leverage Enterprise Architecture to successfully complete the steps and tasks described in the DPBoK Standard while managing risks.

</details>

<a id="1.3"></a>
## 1.3 战略

原文：1.3. Strategy。PDF 物理页：[20, 21]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

在考察 DPBoK 标准、建设数字企业这一主题，以及企业架构与 TOGAF 标准如何具体支持数字企业时，可以明显看到调整关注重点会有所帮助。由此，本文档明确阐述了两项贯穿全文的企业架构策略：

- **前瞻策略（peek-ahead strategy）**

  第一项策略，是从“架构师批准之后才做”转向“用架构使能来做”。使能的形式是：只提供刚好足够的风险、标准与最佳实践指导，以便按语境交付最小可行数字产品，同时向前看，确保能够平滑过渡到下一个语境。这并非要阻止进展，而是要确保今天所做的决策对潜在问题与困难有恰当的理解。承担架构师角色的人即可实施该策略。即使在 DPBoK 标准的个人/创始人语境中，个人/创始人也在提供原本由企业架构师提供的业务分析，即便采用的是临时、随需的方式。

- **企业架构即服务策略（Enterprise Architecture as services strategy）**

  第二项策略进一步支持使能：从产出架构并据此设置关卡，转向按需开发刚好足够的架构，以支撑数字工作的运营节奏。这通过由承担企业架构师角色的人提供的企业架构服务交付模式来实现，并以“使能—咨询”的方式进行。这一点尤其重要：

  - 在团队之团队层面，架构师可以发挥作用，改善跨团队沟通，降低团队协作的认知负荷；
  - 在更大的组织中，作为创新/孵化策略的一部分，向创始人/团队提供咨询服务。

这两项策略连同本文档所载的指导，将提高任何组织中数字企业成功的概率。

尾注

[1] DPBoK 标准，第 5.2 节。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 前瞻策略用刚好足够的风险、标准与实践指导，使当前最小可行产品能向下一语境平滑过渡。
  - 适用边界：角色可以由创始人承担；本段不要求创建独立架构部门或先审批后试验。
  - PDF 20：The first strategy is to move from a “do it if, and after, the architect says OK” to a “do it with the architecture enablement” approach. Enablement comes in the form of using just enough guidance on risks, standards, and best practices to deliver the minimum viable digital product per context, while looking ahead to ensure that a smooth transition to the next context is enabled. This is not meant to stop progress, but rather to ensure that decisions are taken today with appropriate understanding of potential problems and difficulties. This strategy can be done by someone undertaking the role of an architect. Even in the Individual/Founder Context of the DPBoK Standard, the individual/founder provides the business analysis delivered by an Enterprise Architect, even if it is done in an ad hoc fashion.
- 架构以按需服务支持数字工作的运营节奏，由承担架构师角色的人提供使能与咨询。
  - 适用边界：不能把服务模型实现成默认阻断交付的关卡。
  - PDF 21：The second strategy further supports enablement by moving from producing architectures, and gating progress, to developing just enough architecture on demand to support the operations tempo of the digital effort. This is accomplished through an Enterprise Architecture services delivery model provided by those undertaking the Enterprise Architect role. This is done in an enabling-consulting fashion. This is especially significant:

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0020:c004 · PDF 20**

1.3. Strategy

**G217:p0020:c005 · PDF 20**

In looking at the DPBoK Standard, the subject of building a digital enterprise, and how Enterprise Architecture and the TOGAF Standard specifically support the digital enterprise, it became obvious that a shift in focus would be useful. This resulted in spelling out two specific Enterprise Architecture strategies that are used throughout this document:

**G217:p0020:c006 · PDF 20**

• The peek-ahead strategy

**G217:p0020:c007 · PDF 20**

The first strategy is to move from a “do it if, and after, the architect says OK” to a “do it with the architecture enablement” approach. Enablement comes in the form of using just enough guidance on risks, standards, and best practices to deliver the minimum viable digital product per context, while looking ahead to ensure that a smooth transition to the next context is enabled. This is not meant to stop progress, but rather to ensure that decisions are taken today with appropriate understanding of potential problems and difficulties. This strategy can be done by someone undertaking the role of an architect. Even in the Individual/Founder Context of the DPBoK Standard, the individual/founder provides the business analysis delivered by an Enterprise Architect, even if it is done in an ad hoc fashion.

**G217:p0021:c000 · PDF 21**

• Enterprise Architecture as services strategy

**G217:p0021:c001 · PDF 21**

The second strategy further supports enablement by moving from producing architectures, and gating progress, to developing just enough architecture on demand to support the operations tempo of the digital effort. This is accomplished through an Enterprise Architecture services delivery model provided by those undertaking the Enterprise Architect role. This is done in an enabling-consulting fashion. This is especially significant:

**G217:p0021:c002 · PDF 21**

◦At the Team of Teams level where the architect can serve to improve cross-team

**G217:p0021:c003 · PDF 21**

communication and reduce the cognitive load of teams working together

**G217:p0021:c004 · PDF 21**

◦In a larger organization that offers consultative services to founders/teams as part of an

**G217:p0021:c005 · PDF 21**

innovation/incubation strategy

**G217:p0021:c006 · PDF 21**

These two strategies along with the guidance contained in this document will improve the probability of success for the digital enterprise in any organization.

**G217:p0021:c007 · PDF 21**

Endnotes

**G217:p0021:c008 · PDF 21**

[1] The DPBoK Standard, Section 5.2.

</details>

<a id="2"></a>
## 2. 为什么 TOGAF 标准支持数字企业

原文：2. Why the TOGAF Standard Supports the Digital Enterprise。PDF 物理页：[22]。状态：`section-container`。

本节仅为原文标题，正文见所属子节。


### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0022:c000 · PDF 22**

2. Why the TOGAF Standard Supports the Digital

**G217:p0022:c001 · PDF 22**

Enterprise

</details>

<a id="2.1"></a>
## 2.1 引言

原文：2.1. Introduction。PDF 物理页：[22, 23]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

TOGAF 标准开始演进的时代，服务器硬件与网络设备需要采购，数据中心的场地、电力与制冷需要规划，产品许可证需要谈判并购买。技术基础设施必须在项目早期就得到良好规划并部署到位，以便留出足够的采购提前期。如今，购置与搭建硬件和网络已有替代方案，许多产品许可证也已是开源许可。

环境虽已改变，交付 IT 能力所需的业务职能依然存在。这些职能仍然需要理解业务流程背后的概念，并需要一种方法或方法论来完成其相关任务。TOGAF 标准与企业架构对于理解业务职能以及人们为支撑这些职能必须完成的任务仍然具有相关性。

企业之所以需要演化为数字企业，可追溯到多种驱动因素，其中原文称较不重要的一项是技术的快速变化——这些技术催生了新的工作、社交与娱乐方式。企业架构能力与 TOGAF 标准支持敏捷软件交付环境。应把企业架构视为对敏捷环境的支持与使能：通过就多个领域提供洞见，使敏捷环境更快、更容易地交付并增强数字产品与服务，这些领域包括：

- 以连贯、相互关联的方式反应式地管理冲刺所产生的技术债务
- 主动管理技术债务并预判敏捷开发需求，具体包括：
  - 识别有助于缩短敏捷开发周期的标准与可复用标准组件
  - 以适当的治理或护栏监督组件的复用
- 管理成熟的数字产品并实现运营卓越，具体包括：
  - 使用 TOGAF 架构开发方法（ADM）简化数字生态中的复杂性
  - 建立企业架构能力，推动数字产品与服务管理的运营卓越
  - 将敏捷开发方法制度化，使其成为组织所使用的又一种框架

拥有敏捷文化并采用敏捷交付方法，并不必然产生具备敏捷特征的产品。敏捷交付必须在尽早交付上市所带来的业务价值，与利用并连接生态中其他组件（这些组件会为产品增加价值）所带来的未来价值之间取得平衡。因此，构建敏捷骨干的更恰当方法，可能是着眼于企业整体，尤其是在 DPBoK 标准的团队之团队与持久企业语境中——换言之，即 TOGAF 核心概念与 ADM。关于企业架构如何支持使用 TOGAF 标准的敏捷交付，参见《TOGAF® 系列指南：赋能企业敏捷性》[G20F]。通常，最快的交付所采用的解决方案，往往不易与其他特性适配，也难以连接到生态中能为产品增值的其他组件。

重要的是要认识到：组织要成为数字化组织，就必须用能为客户提供竞争对手尚未考虑到的额外价值的产品与服务来改进其数字供给。为此，实验至关重要，即采用迭代的“测试—学习”方法识别新的数字产品。企业架构非常适合支持这种实验，使业务目标与数字愿景保持一致；例如，运用设计思维等技巧。使用架构备选方案来选择不同的潜在实施路线图也是一项优势。关于如何处理架构备选方案的更多细节，可参见 TOGAF 标准——ADM 技术 [C220] 第 9 章“架构备选方案与权衡”。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 敏捷文化和敏捷交付方法不保证产品自身具备适应变化的能力。
  - 适用边界：应权衡早交付的价值与未来连接、复用及生态适配的价值，不能仅以冲刺速度验证架构敏捷性。
  - PDF 22：Having an Agile culture and using Agile delivery methods does not necessarily lead to products with Agile characteristics. Agile delivery must balance the business value of early delivery to market [1] with the future value of leveraging and connecting to other components in the ecosystem that would add value to the product. As a result, a more appropriate approach to developing an Agile backbone may
- 用迭代试验、设计思维及架构备选方案把业务目标与数字愿景相连接。
  - 适用边界：备选路线图应保留权衡；试验不意味着已有确定的目标方案。
  - PDF 23：It is important to consider that for organizations to become digital they must improve their digital offerings with products and services that offer additional value not considered by competitors. To achieve this, experimentation is important, using iterative test-and-learn approaches to identify new digital products. Enterprise Architecture is well-suited to support this experimentation, providing the alignment of business objectives to the digital vision; for example, by applying techniques like design thinking. The use of architecture alternatives to select different potential implementation roadmaps is also a plus. More details on how to handle architecture alternatives can be found in the TOGAF Standard — ADM Techniques [C220], Chapter 9, “Architecture Alternatives and Trade-Off”.

**G217:p0022:c002 · PDF 22**

2.1. Introduction

**G217:p0022:c003 · PDF 22**

The TOGAF Standard began to evolve at a time when server hardware and networking equipment needed to be purchased; data center space, power, and cooling needed to be planned for, and product licenses negotiated and purchased. Technology infrastructure had to be well-planned and in place in the early stages of a project to allow for adequate lead time. Now, there are alternatives to purchasing and staging hardware and networking, and many product licenses are open-source licensing.

**G217:p0022:c004 · PDF 22**

While the environment has changed, the business functions needed to deliver an IT capability still exist. These functions still require an understanding of the concepts behind the business process, and an approach or methodology to accomplish their associated tasks. The TOGAF Standard and Enterprise Architecture are relevant to understand business functions and the tasks that must be completed by people in order to enable the business functions.

**G217:p0022:c005 · PDF 22**

The need for companies to evolve into digital enterprises can be linked to a variety of drivers, least of which is the rapid change in technologies that lend themselves to new ways of working, socializing, and entertaining. The Enterprise Architecture capability and the TOGAF Standard support Agile software delivery environments. An Enterprise Architecture should be seen as supporting and enabling the Agile environment in delivering and enhancing digital products and services quicker and easier by providing insight into various areas; including:

**G217:p0022:c006 · PDF 22**

• Reactively managing technical debt as the result of sprints in a cohesive and connected fashion

**G217:p0022:c007 · PDF 22**

• Proactively managing technical debt and anticipating Agile development needs by:

**G217:p0022:c008 · PDF 22**

◦Identifying standards and reusable standard components that support shortened Agile

**G217:p0022:c009 · PDF 22**

development cycles

**G217:p0022:c010 · PDF 22**

◦Appropriate governance or guardrails to oversee the reuse of components

**G217:p0022:c011 · PDF 22**

• Managing matured digital products and delivering operational excellence by:

**G217:p0022:c012 · PDF 22**

◦Simplifying complexity in the digital ecosystem using the TOGAF Architecture Development

**G217:p0022:c013 · PDF 22**

Method (ADM)

**G217:p0022:c014 · PDF 22**

◦Establishing an Enterprise Architecture capability that drives operational excellence in the

**G217:p0022:c015 · PDF 22**

management of digital products and services

**G217:p0022:c016 · PDF 22**

◦Institutionalizing Agile development methods by enabling them as another framework used in

**G217:p0022:c017 · PDF 22**

the organization

**G217:p0022:c018 · PDF 22**

Having an Agile culture and using Agile delivery methods does not necessarily lead to products with Agile characteristics. Agile delivery must balance the business value of early delivery to market [1] with the future value of leveraging and connecting to other components in the ecosystem that would add value to the product. As a result, a more appropriate approach to developing an Agile backbone may

**G217:p0023:c000 · PDF 23**

be one that looks at the enterprise, particularly in the Team of Teams and Enduring Enterprise contexts of the DPBoK Standard. In other words, the TOGAF Core Concepts and ADM. For more information on Enterprise Architecture supporting Agile delivery using the TOGAF Standard; see the TOGAF® Series Guide: Enabling Enterprise Agility [G20F]. Often the quickest delivery involves solutions that are not easily adaptable with other features and difficult to connect to other components in the ecosystem that would add value to the product.

**G217:p0023:c001 · PDF 23**

It is important to consider that for organizations to become digital they must improve their digital offerings with products and services that offer additional value not considered by competitors. To achieve this, experimentation is important, using iterative test-and-learn approaches to identify new digital products. Enterprise Architecture is well-suited to support this experimentation, providing the alignment of business objectives to the digital vision; for example, by applying techniques like design thinking. The use of architecture alternatives to select different potential implementation roadmaps is also a plus. More details on how to handle architecture alternatives can be found in the TOGAF Standard — ADM Techniques [C220], Chapter 9, “Architecture Alternatives and Trade-Off”.

</details>

<a id="2.2"></a>
## 2.2 反应式管理技术债务

原文：2.2. Reactively Managing Technical Debt。PDF 物理页：[23, 24]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

> “技术债务（也称设计债务或代码债务）是软件开发中的一个概念，反映因现在选择简单方案而非采用耗时更长的更好方法而产生的额外返工隐含成本。”——Wikipedia
>
> “问题不仅在于典型的临时方案、今天有利而明天昂贵的捷径造成技术债务（在‘功能工厂’中并不少见）；还有一种技术债务是 Scrum 团队在更了解要解决的问题时被动产生的：产品团队今天可能更倾向于与六个月前实现的方案不同的方案，或升级‘完成’的定义，从而在以往产品增量中引入返工。无论从哪个角度看，都无法回避，Scrum 也并未提供银弹。”——Scrum.org

敏捷开发中可由企业架构方法提供帮助的一个领域，是技术与架构债务的管理。技术债务并非坏事，但它可能以未来延迟与不灵活为代价换取短期收益。

一旦产生技术债务，就必须处理——这可视为**反应式管理**技术债务。许多技术债务问题可在产品待办列表中处理：确立“技术债务被跟踪、并作为冲刺事项持续偿还”的原则。然而，另一些技术债务因其极其复杂或横切性质，更适合从企业视角处理，可用企业架构和/或 TOGAF 标准的方法管理；不需要企业架构的技术债务，则可通过软件或中间件模式缓解，以简化可维护性。此类技术债务也可能出现在技术域。

基于 TOGAF 标准的方法也适合识别因**冗余**产生的技术债务。由于所有产品都基于同一数字生态，多个 Scrum 团队可能遇到相同的“架构路障”。使用 TOGAF ADM 作为企业架构方法，应能发现这些重叠，不仅解决特定产品架构的问题，还能洞察数字生态本身的基本性、系统性缺陷，使团队能够开发或获取解决多个产品共性问题的方案。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 技术债务可来自权宜决策，也可来自团队学习后的方案变化或完成定义升级。
  - 适用边界：不能把所有债务归因为团队失误。
  - PDF 23：“The issue is that there is not just the typical hack, the technical shortcut that is beneficial today, but expensive tomorrow that creates technical debt. (A not uncommon tactic in feature factories.) There is also a kind of technical debt that is passively created when the Scrum team learns more about the problem it is trying to solve. Today, the product team might prefer a different solution by comparison to the one the team implemented just six months ago. Or, the product team upgrades the definition of “done”, thus introducing rework in former product increments. No matter from what angle you look at the problem, you cannot escape it, and Scrum does not offer a silver bullet either.” Scrum.org™ [Scrum]
- 产品内部债务可以进入待办并持续偿还；复杂、横切的债务可能需要企业架构介入。
  - 适用边界：本段不要求所有技术债务升级为企业级治理；软件和中间件模式仍可能是适当手段。
  - PDF 23：Once you incur technical debt, you have to do something with it. This can be considered as reactively managing technical debt. Many technical debt issues can be dealt with in the product backlog by having the principle that technical debt is tracked and continually paid down as part of items in the sprints. However, there are other forms of technical debt that are better dealt with from an enterprise perspective because of their extremely complex or cross-cutting nature, which may be better managed using Enterprise Architecture and/or following the approaches in the TOGAF Standard. Other forms of technical debt that do not require Enterprise Architecture or approaches from the TOGAF Standard are technical debts that can be mitigated by following software or middleware patterns. This approach might be preferred to simplify maintainability. Technical debt of this nature can also appear in the technology domain.
- 跨产品重复遇到的架构障碍可揭示生态的系统性缺陷，应检查共同方案的价值。
  - 适用边界：须先有跨产品重叠证据；不能因产品同属一个企业就强制共享所有实现。
  - PDF 24：An approach based on the TOGAF Standard is also well-suited to identifying technical debt incurred due to redundancy. As all products are based in the same digital ecosystem, multiple Scrum teams may have run into the same “architecture roadblocks”. Using the TOGAF ADM as an Enterprise Architecture approach should uncover these overlaps and not only address the issues specific to particular product architectures, but also gain insight into fundamental and systematic deficiencies in the digital ecosystem itself. This can enable teams to develop or acquire solutions to address issues from which multiple products might suffer.

**G217:p0023:c002 · PDF 23**

2.2. Reactively Managing Technical Debt

**G217:p0023:c003 · PDF 23**

“Technical debt (also known as design debt or code debt) is a concept in software development that reflects the implied cost of additional rework caused by choosing an easy solution now instead of using a better approach that would take longer.” Wikipedia® [Wikipedia]

**G217:p0023:c004 · PDF 23**

“The issue is that there is not just the typical hack, the technical shortcut that is beneficial today, but expensive tomorrow that creates technical debt. (A not uncommon tactic in feature factories.) There is also a kind of technical debt that is passively created when the Scrum team learns more about the problem it is trying to solve. Today, the product team might prefer a different solution by comparison to the one the team implemented just six months ago. Or, the product team upgrades the definition of “done”, thus introducing rework in former product increments. No matter from what angle you look at the problem, you cannot escape it, and Scrum does not offer a silver bullet either.” Scrum.org™ [Scrum]

**G217:p0023:c005 · PDF 23**

One area of Agile development that can be helped by an Enterprise Architecture approach is the management of technical and architectural debt. Technical debt is not bad, but a debt may bring a

**G217:p0023:c006 · PDF 23**

[2]

**G217:p0023:c007 · PDF 23**

short-term benefit at the cost of future delay and inflexibility in bringing new features to market.

**G217:p0023:c008 · PDF 23**

Once you incur technical debt, you have to do something with it. This can be considered as reactively managing technical debt. Many technical debt issues can be dealt with in the product backlog by having the principle that technical debt is tracked and continually paid down as part of items in the sprints. However, there are other forms of technical debt that are better dealt with from an enterprise perspective because of their extremely complex or cross-cutting nature, which may be better managed using Enterprise Architecture and/or following the approaches in the TOGAF Standard. Other forms of technical debt that do not require Enterprise Architecture or approaches from the TOGAF Standard are technical debts that can be mitigated by following software or middleware patterns. This approach might be preferred to simplify maintainability. Technical debt of this nature can also appear in the technology domain.

**G217:p0024:c000 · PDF 24**

An approach based on the TOGAF Standard is also well-suited to identifying technical debt incurred due to redundancy. As all products are based in the same digital ecosystem, multiple Scrum teams may have run into the same “architecture roadblocks”. Using the TOGAF ADM as an Enterprise Architecture approach should uncover these overlaps and not only address the issues specific to particular product architectures, but also gain insight into fundamental and systematic deficiencies in the digital ecosystem itself. This can enable teams to develop or acquire solutions to address issues from which multiple products might suffer.

</details>

<a id="2.3"></a>
## 2.3 主动式管理技术债务

原文：2.3. Proactively Managing Technical Debt。PDF 物理页：[24, 25]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

原则上，技术债务可以避免，但实践中总会产生；不过组织承担债务的数量可以主动管理。TOGAF 标准有多项特性，在企业层面应用时有助于主动管理基础设施。《数字转型战略到实施》[W170] 把债务描述为：

> “期望状态与当前状态之间的差距是一种负债——企业承担的债务。当权衡决策导致工作待办增加时，架构师与企业就是在有意扩大当前与目标状态之间的鸿沟——债务增加。利用成熟度与服务设计的评估，从业者约束并引导企业避免在权衡决策中失足；它还提供洞见，促使企业让所有技能纵向参与——人力资源、财务、产品与服务线、战略等。”

用 TOGAF 标准制定主动管理技术债务的策略时，应考虑：

- **标准**包括内部约定的系统实现方式，其中一些有助于主动管理技术债务。有些标准由法规驱动并接受审计（如 GDPR、SWIFT、巴塞尔 II/III），其他作为最佳实践采纳。无论来源如何，一旦采纳为标准，就可横切产品域乃至整个生态。识别标准并管理其生命周期，可减少 Scrum 团队的猜测；除识别、沟通并让标准可见外，企业架构师还应随时协助 Scrum 团队使用标准。在僵化文化中，正式架构委员会审查架构与已实施系统中的标准使用；而在敏捷开发环境中，架构委员会更像“护栏”，确保系统避免难以逆转或导致集成与运营卓越问题的实现决策。非内部开发的组件也有标准与标准生命周期，会影响数字产品的可维护性。
- **架构存储库与企业连续体**是数字企业的资产，敏捷交付团队可用其做出更明智的设计选择，并复用公司内已验证的设计。尽管敏捷流程不强调文档，TOGAF 标准很大部分确实聚焦记录与分析环境；产品架构可能使用非本环境开发、管理或控制的组件，企业连续体提供了分类与组织组件的方式，增强数字产品的可持续性。
- **数字从业者**关注支撑数字企业的能力（如《数字转型的七个杠杆》[W17D] 所述），不仅是数字产品与服务及 IT 交付层。TOGAF 标准技术可应用于产品架构，如利益相关者管理、业务转型就绪评估、风险管理及 DPBoK 标准所述的其他胜任力领域。


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 技术债务在实践中不可完全避免，但可主动管理承担量。
  - 适用边界：主动管理不意味着无债务，也不意味着所有差距均立即清偿。
  - PDF 24：In principle, technical debt is avoidable, but in practice it is incurred. However, the amount of debt that an organization takes on can be proactively managed. The TOGAF Standard has a number of features that, when applied at the enterprise level, can help in the proactive management of the infrastructure. In Digital Transformation Strategy to Implementation using The Open Group Standards [W170], debt is described as:
- 标准需要识别、沟通、可见性、生命周期管理及使用支持；敏捷架构委员会可提供护栏。
  - 适用边界：不能仅发布标准清单而不支持团队，或把护栏等同于每次变更都集中审批。
  - PDF 24：Some standards are driven by government regulations and are subject to audit, such as General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), SWIFT®, BASEL II, and BASEL III. Other standards are adopted as best practices. Regardless of their origin, once adopted as a standard they can cut across product domains and often the entire ecosystem. Identifying standards and managing the lifecycle of the standards removes some of the guess work for Scrum teams. Beyond identifying the standards used, communicating them, and making them visible, the Enterprise Architect stands ready to assist Scrum teams in using the standards. In a rigid culture, a formalized Architecture Board examines the use of standards in architectures and implemented systems. However, in Agile development environments, the architecture board acts more like guardrails to ensure the systems avoid implementation decisions that are difficult and expensive to reverse, or that can lead to integration and operational excellence issues. Components used in solutions that are not internally developed will have standards and a standards lifecycle which will impact the maintainability of digital products.
- 架构存储库与企业连续体帮助分类、复用设计及管理外部组件对可持续性的影响。
  - 适用边界：数字产品依赖不一定由本企业开发、管理或控制。
  - PDF 25：While Agile processes do not focus on documentation, a large part of the TOGAF Standard does focus on documenting and analyzing the environment. Product architectures can make use of components that are not developed, managed, or controlled by the environment, and the Enterprise Continuum provides a way to classify and organize components that enhance the sustainability of a digital product.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0024:c001 · PDF 24**

2.3. Proactively Managing Technical Debt

**G217:p0024:c002 · PDF 24**

In principle, technical debt is avoidable, but in practice it is incurred. However, the amount of debt that an organization takes on can be proactively managed. The TOGAF Standard has a number of features that, when applied at the enterprise level, can help in the proactive management of the infrastructure. In Digital Transformation Strategy to Implementation using The Open Group Standards [W170], debt is described as:

**G217:p0024:c003 · PDF 24**

“The gap between desired state and current state is a liability – a debt incurred by the enterprise. When trade-off decisions result in adding to the backlog of work, the architect and the enterprise are wilfully increasing the chasm between current and target state – an increase in debt. Using assessments from maturity and service design, the practitioner constrains and guides the enterprise from tripping up during trade-off decisions. It also provides insights and compels the enterprise to involve all skill verticals – Human Resources, Finance, Product and Service lines, Strategy, etc.”

**G217:p0024:c004 · PDF 24**

When creating a strategy to proactively manage technical debt by using the TOGAF Standard, consider the following:

**G217:p0024:c005 · PDF 24**

• Standards include internally agreed upon ways of implementing systems and some of those can help to proactively manage technical debt

**G217:p0024:c006 · PDF 24**

Some standards are driven by government regulations and are subject to audit, such as General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR), SWIFT®, BASEL II, and BASEL III. Other standards are adopted as best practices. Regardless of their origin, once adopted as a standard they can cut across product domains and often the entire ecosystem. Identifying standards and managing the lifecycle of the standards removes some of the guess work for Scrum teams. Beyond identifying the standards used, communicating them, and making them visible, the Enterprise Architect stands ready to assist Scrum teams in using the standards. In a rigid culture, a formalized Architecture Board examines the use of standards in architectures and implemented systems. However, in Agile development environments, the architecture board acts more like guardrails to ensure the systems avoid implementation decisions that are difficult and expensive to reverse, or that can lead to integration and operational excellence issues. Components used in solutions that are not internally developed will have standards and a standards lifecycle which will impact the maintainability of digital products.

**G217:p0024:c007 · PDF 24**

• An Architecture Repository and Enterprise Continuum are assets to the digital enterprise that Agile delivery teams can use to make more informed design choices and to use proven designs from their

**G217:p0025:c000 · PDF 25**

own company

**G217:p0025:c001 · PDF 25**

While Agile processes do not focus on documentation, a large part of the TOGAF Standard does focus on documenting and analyzing the environment. Product architectures can make use of components that are not developed, managed, or controlled by the environment, and the Enterprise Continuum provides a way to classify and organize components that enhance the sustainability of a digital product.

**G217:p0025:c002 · PDF 25**

• Digital Practitioners concern themselves with capabilities to support the digital enterprise as described in The Seven Levers of Digital Transformation [W17D], not just the digital products and services, and IT and delivery layers

**G217:p0025:c003 · PDF 25**

TOGAF Standard techniques can be applied to product architectures, such as stakeholder management, business transformation readiness assessment, risk management, and several other competency areas described in the DPBoK Standard.

</details>

<a id="2.4"></a>
## 2.4 成熟的数字产品与运营卓越

原文：2.4. Mature Digital Products and Operational Excellence。PDF 物理页：[25, 26]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

数字产品与服务有从诞生到退役的生命周期，长度取决于其对生态的可行性。敏捷流程的优势在于基于证据的特性测试，这意味着一些实验会成功；对因各种原因失败的产品，其长期可行性无关紧要。真正证明可行、却未必为可持续性或深度集成而设计的产品，可能需要返工以融入更大生态。

新数字产品会触发一连串事件：融入任何技术的产品都在不断演进；若不演进，就会过时。产品与服务的演进由对客户价值的持续增长驱动；价值可从多个视角度量，一个常见视角是产品/服务与其他事物连接的能力。

TOGAF 标准可用于管理与成熟化数字产品，帮助实现运营卓越。但有时这颇具挑战：敏捷流程聚焦交付代码而非文档，而 TOGAF 标准的方法论很大程度围绕制品收集与管理。尽管敏捷主义者可能觉得“不需要架构”，本文档作者坚持认为需要：可能架构未被理解、未被写下，或设计不佳；但只要有系统，就有架构。本文档不规定架构文档的最佳实践，但每份架构描述都可绘制在图 2-1 的矩阵上。

〔图示：图 2-1：文档矩阵示例；参阅本节结构化引用。〕

系统无论大小、简单或复杂、昨天刚发明还是与穿孔卡大型机一样古老，都有架构；架构有描述，可视觉、口头或书面表达。

架构师的工作是“做刚好的架构，在刚好的时间”。TOGAF 标准描述架构描述应包含什么，如利益相关者、关注点、视图（如利益相关者视图以回应其关注点）与视角。架构的形式由其正式程度衡量，与架构的演进状态分开；原文还将这一衡量与完整性、健全性及稳健性的衡量区分开来。明显的问题区是两个极端：

- 系统（及其架构）复杂却无文档（架构不足）；
- 在尚未验证可行性之前，就对每个潜在系统、产品或想法提出“架构强制要求”（架构过度）。

架构师应具备经验，能够在架构环境语境中应用取得最佳结果的架构形式。

图 2-1 图内文字：纵轴由下方“正式描述”向上方“非正式描述”；横轴由左侧“新颖”向右侧“成熟既有”。左上部为“检查→执行→检查”循环；中部为“行动→计划→执行→检查→行动”循环；右下部为“控制→定义→度量→分析→改进→控制”循环。左下及右上斜线区域在原图中没有文字图例。

相关结构化资料：[figure-2-1](../figures/G217/fig2-1.json)。图注和正文引用纳入本节；不由正文比较自动升级图内转录审校。
相关结构化资料：[figure-visually-compared](../figures/assets/G217/fig2-1-p0026.png)。实际查看本地原图，转录两条轴及三个循环。横轴是新颖至成熟既有，不是充分性。图中斜线区域未加不存在的图例。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 对已验证可行的数字产品，检查可持续性与更大生态集成所需的返工。
  - 适用边界：未成功试验与成熟产品的投资判断不同；不能强制每个试验先完成长期集成。
  - PDF 25：Digital products and services have a lifecycle from inception to retirement. The length of the lifecycle is dependent on its viability to its ecosystem. The strength of Agile processes lies in the evidence-based testing of features, which means that some experiments will be a success. For the products that fail for one reason or another the long-term consequence of their viability is not relevant. It is the product that has proven its viability without necessarily being engineered for sustainability or deeply embedded integration, which may require rework to address integration into the larger ecosystem.
- 架构描述的正式程度、演进状态与完整性、健全性、稳健性应分别判断。
  - 适用边界：原句形式含歧义；不能以文档正式或篇幅大直接证明架构充分。
  - PDF 26：The job of the architect is to do just enough architecture, just in time. The TOGAF Standard describes what the architecture description should contain, such as information about stakeholders, concerns, views (for example, the views of stakeholders – to address their concerns), and viewpoints. Separate and apart from the evolutionary state of the architecture is the form of architecture measured by its formality, which is a separate measurement of its completeness, soundness, and robustness. Obvious problem areas are the extremes, when:
- 尚未验证可行性的每个想法都被强制架构化，是本指南指出的过度架构极端。
  - 适用边界：这不取消必要的风险约束；应按环境选择刚好足够的形式。
  - PDF 26：• There is an “architecture mandate” for every potential system, product, or idea before we have even tested its viability (too much architecture)

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0025:c004 · PDF 25**

2.4. Mature Digital Products and Operational Excellence

**G217:p0025:c005 · PDF 25**

Digital products and services have a lifecycle from inception to retirement. The length of the lifecycle is dependent on its viability to its ecosystem. The strength of Agile processes lies in the evidence-based testing of features, which means that some experiments will be a success. For the products that fail for one reason or another the long-term consequence of their viability is not relevant. It is the product that has proven its viability without necessarily being engineered for sustainability or deeply embedded integration, which may require rework to address integration into the larger ecosystem.

**G217:p0025:c006 · PDF 25**

A new digital product triggers a chain of events. Products that incorporate any sort of technology are constantly evolving. If they are not evolving, they are becoming obsolete. The evolution of products and services is driven by ever-increasing value to the customer. Value can be measured from many perspectives, but one common perspective is the capability of the product or service to connect to other things.

**G217:p0025:c007 · PDF 25**

The TOGAF Standard can be used to manage and mature digital products, and to help deliver operational excellence. However, at times it can feel like quite a challenge as Agile processes focus on delivering code over documentation and the TOGAF Standard centers much of its methodology around artifact collection and management.

**G217:p0025:c008 · PDF 25**

Although there can be a feeling amongst agilists that there is no need for architecture, the authors of this document assert that there is. It may be that the architecture is not understood, or written down, or it may be a poorly designed architecture; but as long as there is a system, there is an architecture. Without suggesting a best practice for architecture documentation, each architecture description can be plotted on the matrix described in Figure 2-1.

**G217:p0026:c000 · PDF 26**

Figure 2-1. Example of Documentation Matrix

**G217:p0026:c001 · PDF 26**

A system, whether large, small, simple, complex, just invented yesterday, or as old as the punch card mainframes, has an architecture. And that architecture has a description; we can describe it visually, verbally, or in a written form.

**G217:p0026:c002 · PDF 26**

The job of the architect is to do just enough architecture, just in time. The TOGAF Standard describes what the architecture description should contain, such as information about stakeholders, concerns, views (for example, the views of stakeholders – to address their concerns), and viewpoints. Separate and apart from the evolutionary state of the architecture is the form of architecture measured by its formality, which is a separate measurement of its completeness, soundness, and robustness. Obvious problem areas are the extremes, when:

**G217:p0026:c003 · PDF 26**

• The system (and its architecture) is complex and undocumented (insufficient architecture)

**G217:p0026:c004 · PDF 26**

• There is an “architecture mandate” for every potential system, product, or idea before we have even tested its viability (too much architecture)

**G217:p0026:c005 · PDF 26**

The architect should have the experience to be able to apply a format of architecture that achieves the best result within the context of the architectural environment.

</details>

<a id="2.5"></a>
## 2.5 简化复杂性（TOGAF ADM）

原文：2.5. Simplifying Complexity (The TOGAF ADM)。PDF 物理页：[26, 27]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

数字产品必须适应其不断演进的生态，否则将失去价值；可能变得低效、过时或维护成本过高。构建良好、可适应、可扩展且灵活的生态正是架构的目的。

TOGAF ADM 以企业架构的形式，为理解和描述更大的数字生态提供指导。TOGAF 标准还可用于把生态划分为分段架构，而数字产品则可描述为解决方案架构。

随着数字产品与服务复杂性增加，可以且应当采用更严格的架构形式，以保持数字产品的“敏捷”特性。问题在于应施加多少严格性？数字从业者可以利用 TOGAF 标准中的指导，缓解复杂性造成的持续交付放缓；例如，倡导以严格且结构化的方式开发相对易于集成的可复用组件。反过来，架构师可以选择有助于应对复杂性的 TOGAF 组件，并舍弃对其所处语境而言过度设计的组件。TOGAF 标准指出，应针对较小或较不复杂的环境对其进行调整与“恰当规模裁剪”。

最重要的是，TOGAF 标准聚焦于对客户或最终用户有价值的业务成果，这与数字产品与服务的价值衡量方式一致。然而，“你构建它，你运营它”的敏捷方法在运营上未必高效，尤其是当数字产品开始嵌入技术基础设施的核心运营模式时。TOGAF 标准提供的框架不仅容纳产品架构，还容纳产品所参与的整个能力。

尾注

[1] DPBoK 标准，第 6.6.2.2.9 节“延迟成本”。

[2] DPBoK 标准，第 6.2.2.2.3 节“将开发过程前置”：“在开发过程早期做出的糟糕决策，其负面后果会随时间呈指数级增长，因为在生命周期后期逆转这些决策会越来越困难。”


### 语义规则与适用边界

- 随复杂性增加可以增加架构严谨度，同时取消对当前语境过度设计的 TOGAF 组件。
  - 适用边界：严谨度必须裁剪；不能把成熟产品的要求无条件施加于小型或简单环境。
  - PDF 27：As digital products and services increase in complexity, more rigorous forms of architecture can and should be applied to maintain the “Agile” aspects of digital products. The question becomes how much rigor to apply? Using guidance available in the TOGAF Standard, the Digital Practitioner can mitigate slow-downs in continuous delivery caused by complexity; for example, by evangelizing the rigorous and structured development of reusable components that are relatively easy to integrate. Conversely, the architect can select TOGAF components that help to address complexity and deselect components that are over-engineered for the context in which they are operating. The TOGAF Standard states that it should be adapted and “right-sized” for smaller or less complex environments.
- 架构覆盖客户价值及产品所参与的整项能力，运营责任模式应考虑效率。
  - 适用边界：you build it, you operate it 不是所有环境都高效的普适规定。
  - PDF 27：Most importantly, the TOGAF Standard focuses on business outcomes described as valuable to the customer or end user. This aligns with how the value of digital products and services are measured. However, the Agile approach of “you build it, you operate it” may not be operationally efficient, especially when the digital product begins to embed itself into the core operational model of the technology infrastructure. The TOGAF Standard provides the framework that accommodates not only product architectures but entire capabilities in which the product participates.

### 原文内容单元

<details><summary>展开英文原文及文本块定位</summary>

**G217:p0026:c006 · PDF 26**

2.5. Simplifying Complexity (The TOGAF ADM)

**G217:p0026:c007 · PDF 26**

Digital products must adapt to their evolving ecosystem or they will lose their value. The digital products might become inefficient, obsolete, or too expensive to maintain. A well-constructed ecosystem that is adaptable, scalable, and flexible is the purpose of an architecture.

**G217:p0026:c008 · PDF 26**

The TOGAF ADM provides guidance for understanding and describing the larger digital ecosystem in

**G217:p0027:c000 · PDF 27**

the form of Enterprise Architecture. The TOGAF Standard can also be used to partition the ecosystem into segment architectures, while digital products can be described as solution architectures.

**G217:p0027:c001 · PDF 27**

As digital products and services increase in complexity, more rigorous forms of architecture can and should be applied to maintain the “Agile” aspects of digital products. The question becomes how much rigor to apply? Using guidance available in the TOGAF Standard, the Digital Practitioner can mitigate slow-downs in continuous delivery caused by complexity; for example, by evangelizing the rigorous and structured development of reusable components that are relatively easy to integrate. Conversely, the architect can select TOGAF components that help to address complexity and deselect components that are over-engineered for the context in which they are operating. The TOGAF Standard states that it should be adapted and “right-sized” for smaller or less complex environments.

**G217:p0027:c002 · PDF 27**

Most importantly, the TOGAF Standard focuses on business outcomes described as valuable to the customer or end user. This aligns with how the value of digital products and services are measured. However, the Agile approach of “you build it, you operate it” may not be operationally efficient, especially when the digital product begins to embed itself into the core operational model of the technology infrastructure. The TOGAF Standard provides the framework that accommodates not only product architectures but entire capabilities in which the product participates.

**G217:p0027:c003 · PDF 27**

Endnotes

**G217:p0027:c004 · PDF 27**

[1] The DPBoK Standard, Section 6.6.2.2.9, “Cost of Delay”. [2] The DPBoK Standard, Section 6.2.2.2.3, “Front Load the Development Process”: “Poor decisions made early in the development process have negative consequences that increase exponentially over time because reversing them later in the lifecycle becomes more and more difficult”.

</details>

<a id="3"></a>
## 3. 术语对齐

原文：3. Terminology Alignment。PDF 物理页：[28, 29, 30]。状态：`source-compared-revised`。

TOGAF 标准与 DPBoK 标准使用的术语在定义与用法上并不冲突。不过，正如所料，一方使用的某些术语，在另一方当前并未使用或并不相关。

表 3-1 列出了 DPBoK 标准中使用的、聚焦数字企业的一些关键术语。

**表 3-1：TOGAF 标准与 DPBoK 标准的术语对齐**

| 术语（Term） | DPBoK Term（DPBoK 术语）[1] | TOGAF Term（TOGAF 术语）[2] |
|---|---|---|
| 应用组件<br>（Application Component） | DPBoK 标准以更一般的方式使用“组件”（component）这一术语，因此不存在冲突。 | 与实现结构对齐的应用功能封装，具有模块化与可替换性。它封装自身的行为与数据，提供服务，并通过接口对外提供。<br><br>应用组件通常维护一个数据组件，并由技术组件提供的技术服务使能。 |
| 架构<br>（Architecture） | 两项标准共享同一定义。 | 1. 实体在其环境中的基本概念或属性，以及实现和演进该实体及其相关生命周期过程的治理原则（来源：ISO/IEC/IEEE 42010 [ISO42010]）。<br><br>2. 组件的结构、它们之间的相互关系，以及指导其设计与随时间演进的原则与指南。 |
| 数字企业<br>（Digital Enterprise） | 具备以下特征的企业：<br>1. 创造完全以数字方式交付的数字化产品或服务；例如数字媒体或在线银行。<br>2. 实体产品与服务由客户以数字方式获得；例如在线汽车共享服务。 | TOGAF 没有“数字企业”的专门定义；不过，DPBoK 标准给出的定义属于 TOGAF 标准所定义的企业的一种特殊类型。TOGAF 标准认为，企业是任何具有共同目标、在各种规模上运作的组织集合。 |
| 数字战略<br>（Digital Strategy） | DPBoK 标准未定义数字战略；但它隐含地认为，数字战略是旨在通向成功数字企业的战略。 | TOGAF 标准未定义该术语，因此不存在冲突。 |
| 数字技术<br>（Digital Technology） | 以产品或服务形式存在、可被数字消费以创造或使能业务价值的信息技术。 | TOGAF 标准未定义该术语，因此不存在冲突。 |
| 数字转型<br>（Digital Transformation） | 向数字企业转变的根本性、彻底性变革。 | TOGAF 标准未定义该术语，因此不存在冲突。 |
| 数字化<br>（Digitalization） | 把数字技术应用于企业主价值链，以创造额外业务价值。 | TOGAF 标准未定义该术语，因此不存在冲突。 |
| 数位化<br>（Digitization） | 把模拟信息转换为数字形式。 | TOGAF 标准未定义该术语，因此不存在冲突。 |
| 流程<br>（Process） | 有序、可计数的活动集合；可度量并改进的事件驱动增值序列。 | 使用该术语且不存在冲突。流程表示一组共同达成特定结果的活动，可分解为子流程，并可展示业务能力或服务的运作（下一层细节）；参见 TOGAF 标准——应用 ADM [C220]。两种定义并不矛盾。 |
| 服务<br>（Service） | DPBoK 标准未定义该术语，因此不存在冲突。 | 可重复的活动；构建块可被请求或以其他方式触发执行的离散行为。<br><br>响应参与者或其他服务请求而提供特定功能的行为要素。 |
| 技术组件<br>（Technology Component） | DPBoK 标准以更一般的方式使用“组件”这一术语，因此不存在冲突。 | 1. 技术构建块：一种通用基础设施技术，通过提供技术服务（直接或间接地）支撑并使能应用或数据组件。<br><br>2. 技术基础设施的封装，代表一类技术产品或特定技术产品。 |

以下术语与 TOGAF 标准和 DPBoK 标准都相关，此处给出可用于框定对话的定义：

- **数字标准（或指南）**：帮助企业作为数字企业取得成功的出版物；即主要通过数字方式交付价值（有时称为“数字优先”业务模式）的企业。
- **涌现模型**：一种信息组织方式，其中主题与组织复杂性或企业规模相关联（来源：DPBoK 标准，扩展模型）。
- **原则**：应当由架构满足的、关于意图的定性陈述（来源：TOGAF 标准——ADM 技术，第 2.3 节“架构原则的构成”）。


脚注：
[1] 术语取自 DPBoK 标准第 2 章“定义”，另有说明者除外。
[2] 术语取自 TOGAF 标准——引言与核心概念第 4 章“定义”，另有说明者除外。

### 语义规则与适用边界

- 术语对齐表表达本指南引用版本间的定义及用法关系。
  - 适用边界：某标准未定义某术语不等于证明两个概念完全同义；不能外推至未审阅的新版本。
  - PDF 28：The terminology used in the TOGAF Standard and the DPBoK Standard does not conflict in definition and usage. However, as you might expect, there are terms that are used in one that are either currently not used or not relevant in the other.
- 数字化指用数字技术创造额外业务价值，数位化指把模拟信息转换成数字形式。
  - 适用边界：转换格式不自动构成业务模式转型或新增业务价值。
  - PDF 29：| Digitalization | The application of digital technology to<br>create additional business value within<br>the primary value chain of enterprises. | The term is not defined in the TOGAF<br>Standard; therefore, there is no conflict. |
- 涌现模型按企业复杂性或规模组织信息。
  - 适用边界：它不是对企业必定沿单一时间线成长的预测，也不替代实际语境评估。
  - PDF 30：• Emergence model: An organization of information where topics are related to the organizational complexity or scale of the enterprise (Source: The DPBoK Standard, Scaling Model)

**G217:p0028:c000 · PDF 28**

3. Terminology Alignment

**G217:p0028:c001 · PDF 28**

The terminology used in the TOGAF Standard and the DPBoK Standard does not conflict in definition and usage. However, as you might expect, there are terms that are used in one that are either currently not used or not relevant in the other.

**G217:p0028:c002 · PDF 28**

Table 3-1 provides a list of some of the key terms focused on the digital enterprise and used in the DPBoK Standard.

**G217:p0028:c003 · PDF 28**

Table 3-1. Terminology Alignment between the TOGAF and DPBoK Standards

**G217:p0028:c004 · PDF 28**

| Term | DPBoK Term[1] | TOGAF Term[2] |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Application<br>Component | The term component is used in a more<br>general way in the DPBoK Standard;<br>therefore, there is no conflict. | An encapsulation of application<br>functionality aligned to implementation<br>structure, which is modular and<br>replaceable. It encapsulates its behavior<br>and data, provides services, and makes<br>them available through interfaces.<br>An application component usually<br>maintains a data component. It is<br>enabled by technology services<br>provided by technology components. |
| Architecture | Both standards share the same<br>definition. | 1. [The] fundamental concepts or<br>properties of an entity in its<br>environment and governing principles<br>for the realization and evolution of this<br>entity and its related life cycle<br>processes. (Source: ISO/IEC/IEEE 42010<br>[ISO42010]<br>2. The structure of components, their<br>inter-relationships, and the principles<br>and guidelines governing their design<br>and evolution over time. |

**G217:p0029:c000 · PDF 29**

| Term | DPBoK Term[1] | TOGAF Term[2] |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Digital Enterprise | An enterprise characterized by:<br>1. The creation of digitalized products<br>or services that are delivered fully<br>digitally; e.g., digital media or online<br>banking.<br>2. Where physical products and services<br>are obtained by the customer by digital<br>means; e.g., online car-sharing services. | There is no specific TOGAF definition<br>for digital enterprise; however, the<br>definition provided in the DPBoK<br>Standard is a special type of enterprise,<br>as defined in the TOGAF Standard. The<br>TOGAF Standard considers an<br>enterprise to be any collection of<br>organizations that have common goals,<br>operating at all scales. |
| Digital Strategy | The DPBoK Standard does not define<br>digital strategy; however, it implies that<br>a digital strategy is one which aspires to<br>lead to a successful digital enterprise. | The term is not defined in the TOGAF<br>Standard; therefore, there is no conflict. |
| Digital Technology | IT in the form of a product or service<br>that is digitally consumable to create or<br>enable business value. | The term is not defined in the TOGAF<br>Standard; therefore, there is no conflict. |
| Digital<br>Transformation | The radical, fundamental change<br>toward becoming a digital enterprise. | The term is not defined in the TOGAF<br>Standard; therefore, there is no conflict. |
| Digitalization | The application of digital technology to<br>create additional business value within<br>the primary value chain of enterprises. | The term is not defined in the TOGAF<br>Standard; therefore, there is no conflict. |
| Digitization | The conversion of analog information<br>into digital form. | The term is not defined in the TOGAF<br>Standard; therefore, there is no conflict. |

A process represents a<br>sequence of activities that together<br>achieve a specified outcome, can be<br>decomposed into sub-processes, and<br>can show the operation of a business<br>capability or service (at the next level of<br>detail); see the TOGAF Standard —<br>Applying the ADM [C220]. The<br>definitions do not contradict each other. |

**G217:p0030:c001 · PDF 30**

The following terms are relevant to both the TOGAF Standard and the DPBoK Standard, and definitions are provided that can be used to frame conversations:

**G217:p0030:c002 · PDF 30**

• Digital standard (or guide): A publication that helps an enterprise succeed as a digital enterprise; i.e., one that primarily delivers value through digital means (sometimes called a “digital-first” business model)

**G217:p0030:c003 · PDF 30**

• Emergence model: An organization of information where topics are related to the organizational complexity or scale of the enterprise (Source: The DPBoK Standard, Scaling Model)

**G217:p0030:c004 · PDF 30**

• Principle: A qualitative statement of intent that should be met by the architecture (Source: TOGAF Standard — ADM Techniques, Section 2.3: “Components of Architecture Principles”)

**G217:p0030:c005 · PDF 30**

Endnotes

## 4. TOGAF 标准如何支持数字企业

原文：4. How the TOGAF Standard Supports the Digital Enterprise。PDF 物理页：[31]。状态：`section-container`。

本节仅为原文标题，正文见所属子节。


### 原文内容单元

4. How the TOGAF Standard Supports the Digital

Enterprise
